We consider the following claim:

Claim 1
.

We have the following isomorphisms of Selmer groups over
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
respectively:

(154)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}),\qquad\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}).

Proof of Claim
1
.

We provide the proof of the isomorphism of
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-modules. The proof for the isomorphism of
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-modules can be similarly deduced. The hypothesis

p

-Dist-GSp4
crucially comes into play. Without this assumption (e.g. in the case of trivial zeroes), such an equality of divisors need not always hold. One can use the inflation-restriction map and the snake lemma to show that the cokernel of the natural inclusion
\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}})
is an
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-submodule of

(155)

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Gamma_{p}\,H^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(I_{p},\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)\right).

Here
\Gamma_{p}
, which is isomorphic to
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
, denotes the Galois group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
of the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We let the Frobenius
\mathrm{Frob}_{p}
denote a topological generator of
\Gamma_{p}
. To prove the claim, it suffices to show that the Pontryagin dual of the
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-module appearing in equation (
155
) is zero. Consider the following short exact sequence of
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-modules.

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Gamma_{p}\,H^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(I_{p},\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)\right)^{\vee}\rightarrow H^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(I_{p},\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Frob}_{p}-1}

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(I_{p},\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)^{\vee}

(156)

\displaystyle\underbrace{\rightarrow H^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)^{\vee}}_{0}\rightarrow 0.

Hypothesis

p

-Dist-GSp4
and
[
28
, Corollary 3.1.1]
let us conclude that
H^{0}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)~=~0
. An application of Nakayama’s lemma would tell us that a surjective endomorphism of a finitely generated
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-module must be an isomorphism. Consequently, the kernel of
\mathrm{Frob}_{p}-1
in equation (
5.3
), and hence the
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-module appearing in equation (
155
), equals zero. The claim follows.
∎

We consider the continuous
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-algebra homomorphism:

\displaystyle\varpi_{3,2}:{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(\sqrt{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma)\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}(\gamma)^{-1}}\right)^{-1},\qquad\forall\ \gamma\in\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}.

By the construction of the ring homomorphism, we have a natural isomorphism
\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}\cong\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}[\ker{\varpi_{3,2}}]
of discrete
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[G_{S}]
-modules. Note that
\ker(\varpi_{3,2})
is a principal ideal in
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
; a generator can be given by

\gamma_{0}-\left(\sqrt{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}(\gamma_{0})\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}(\gamma_{0})^{-1}}\right)^{-1},

where
\gamma_{0}
is a topological generator of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}
. Just as in equation (
5.2
), an application of
[
28
, Proposition 3.4]
along with the hypotheses
Mult-Free
and

p

-Dist-GSp4
will provide us the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G_{S},\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G_{S},\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})[\ker(\varpi_{3,2})]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}\hskip 8.19447pt}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong\hskip 16.38895pt}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)[\ker(\varpi_{3,2})]}

Just as in equation (
145
), we have an exact control theorem for the ring homomorphism
\varpi_{3,2}
, providing the following isomorphism of discrete
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-modules:

(161)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}})\cong\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})[\ker{\varpi_{3,2}}].

Claim 2
.

The
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module
\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}
has no non-trivial pseudonull submodules.

Proof of Claim
2
.

We now consider a few observations to show that all the hypotheses of
[
30
, Proposition 4.1.1]
are satisfied.

•

Since
\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}
is co-free as a
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module, the hypothesis labelled
\mathrm{RFX}(D)
in
[
30
]
is automatically satisfied. Similarly, since
\rho_{\boldsymbol{4},3}
is a cyclotomic twist deformation, the hypotheses labelled
\mathrm{LOC}^{(1)}_{\nu}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})
and
\mathrm{LOC}^{(2)}_{\nu}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})
in
[
30
]
are satisfied for all non-archimedean primes
\nu
.

•

The local condition at
p
defining the Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})
is given by the
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module:

(162)

\displaystyle\ker\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}\right)\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}}\right)\right)

This
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module in turn is a quotient of the
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module
H^{1}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}\right)
. Note that the second local Galois cohomology group
H^{2}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}\right)
equals zero. This is because on the one hand, by
[
28
, Proposition 5.1]
,
it is a co-reflexive
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module; while on the other hand, since the Galois representation
\rho_{\boldsymbol{4},3}
is a cyclotomic twist deformation, using local duality lets us conclude that it is a co-torsion
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module. Applying
[
28
, Propsosition 5.3]
lets us conclude that
H^{1}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}\right)
is an
almost divisible

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module (following the terminology of
[
30
]
).

Similarly, for all non-archimedean primes
\nu\neq p
, the local condition at a prime
\nu\neq p
defining the Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})
is equal to

. As earlier, using the fact that the Galois representation
\rho_{\boldsymbol{4},3}
is a cyclotomic twist deformation, along with local Tate duality, lets us conclude that
H^{2}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{Fil}^{+}}\right)
equals zero. Applying
[
28
, Propsosition 5.3]
once again lets us conclude that
H^{1}\left(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\nu}/\mathbb{Q}_{\nu}),\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}\right)
is an
almost divisible

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module (following the terminology of
[
30
]
).

Since the local condition at
p
, given in equation (
162
), is an
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module quotient of the Galois cohomology group

is also an
almost divisible

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module. One can thus conclude that the entire local condition defining the non-primitive strict Selmer group
\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})
is
almost divisible
.

•

As indicated in the proof of Proposition
5.1
, the ring homomorphisms
\varpi_{f}:{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F}}\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{f_{0}}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

(163)

Equation (
163
) and the hypothesis
Tor
now lets us conclude that
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-module

) then lets us conclude that the

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rank}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}\mathrm{Sel}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{\mathrm{str}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}=0.

Since

would give us the following equality of ranks for the first local Galois cohomology group

(165)

Combining the torsion-ness of the

Combing the equality of ranks in equations (

), (

) and (

•

Since we are looking at the non-primitive Selmer group, the local condition at the prime

is zero. Note also that

One can now use

, Proposition 4.1.1]

Claim 3
.

in the ring

.

1
and the hypothesis of Corollary

will provide us the following inequality of divisors in

Then, Conjectures

and

hold. That is, we obtain the following equality of divisors in

along with the following equality of divisors in
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
:

(175)

.

Since the Galois representation

is a cyclotomic twist deformation, one can use
[

, Proposition 4.1]
to conclude that as an
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}

is torsion. In fact, it is annihilated by a monic polynomial, say
\beta
, in
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![x]\!]
, if we identify
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
with
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![x]\!]
, by identifying a topological generator
\gamma_{0}
of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}
with
x+1
.

The action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on the rank one
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
-module
\mathscr{L}^{*}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{quot}}
, and hence on
\mathscr{D}^{*}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}},\mathrm{quot}}
, is given by the character:

(186)

\displaystyle\chi_{0}\varepsilon\dfrac{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}}}\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{G}}}\right)^{-1}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}^{\times},

where

•

\chi_{0}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}^{\times}
is a finite-order character,

•

\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,\mathrm{cyc}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}[\![x_{\mathcal{F}}]\!]\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}^{\times}
and
\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p,\mathrm{cyc}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}[\![x_{\mathcal{G}}]\!]\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}^{\times}
are the tautological characters,

•

\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}}
and
\epsilon_{\mathcal{G}}
are unramified characters of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
characterized by the properties that
\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{p})=a_{p}(\mathcal{F})
and
\epsilon_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{p})=a_{p}(\mathcal{G})
.

Since the classical specializations of the
U_{p}
-eigenvalue of Hida families varies with weight, one can conclude that
a_{p}(\mathcal{F})
and
a_{p}(\mathcal{G})
(respectively) are elements of
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{G}}
(respectively), are non-constant and hence do not belong to
{\mathcal{O}}
. Note also that the extension
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is totally ramified. Consequently, the restriction of the character in equation (
186
) to the subgroup
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}}))
equals
\psi_{0}\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\epsilon_{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}
for some finite order character
\psi_{0}
. Since
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F}}\cap{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{O}}
, the character
\psi_{0}\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\epsilon_{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}
of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}}))
must be of infinite order, and hence non-trivial. This observation provides a non-zero element in
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
that annihilates
H^{0}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathscr{D}^{*}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{quot}}\right)^{\vee}
, and hence a non-zero annihilator
r
of the
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module in equation (
5.4
). Since
\mathrm{Ann}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]}H^{0}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathscr{D}^{*}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{quot}}\right)^{\vee}\supseteq(\beta,r)
,
the annihilator ideal in
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
must have height
\geq 2
. Thus, the
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module
H^{0}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathscr{D}^{*}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{quot}}\right)^{\vee}
must be pseudo-null. The claim now follows from a direct application of
[
28
, Proposition 5.7]
.

∎

We can now deduce the following short exact sequence from the long exact sequence in equation (
5.4
), Claim
4
and the fact that
\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},(i)}})^{\vee}
is a co-torsion
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-module for each
i\in\{1,2\}
:

(187)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\ \dfrac{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{sum}}^{+}}}{\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!],\mathrm{Fil}_{(i)}^{+}}}\right)^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},\mathrm{sum}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},(i)}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}\rightarrow 0.

This, in turn, allows us to deduce the following inclusions of torsion
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-modules:

(188)

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},\mathrm{sum}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}\right)_{\mathrm{tor}}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},(1)}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee},

(189)

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},\mathrm{sum}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}\right)_{\mathrm{tor}}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},(2)}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}.

Hypothesis
GCD
now lets us conclude that the
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
torsion-submodule
\left(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},\mathrm{sum}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}\right)_{\mathrm{tor}}
is pseudo-null. Since the localization of the ring
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
at a height one prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
is a DVR, one can use the structure theorem for DVRs. Equation (
183
) allows us to deduce the following isomorphism for all height one prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}
in
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
:

(190)

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},\mathrm{sum}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}\right)_{\mathfrak{p}}\cong\left({\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]\right)_{\mathfrak{p}}.

The theorem now follows from equation (
190
) and the surjections of
{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]
-modules:

\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},\mathrm{sum}}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\mathrm{str},(i)}(\mathscr{D}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}[\![\Gamma_{\mathrm{cyc}}]\!]})^{\vee},\qquad i\in\{1,2\},

given in equation (
187
).

Remark 5.3
.

The proof of theorem
3
is motivated by
[
58
, Proposition 4.1]
, which is obtained under more restrictive hypotheses than ours. This necessitates adapting the proof of Claim
4
in our setting since we are working with fewer hypotheses than
[
58
]
.

Appendix A
Miscellaneous results

In this appendix, we collect proofs of a few miscellaneous results, whose proofs will be well-known to the experts. We have included them for the ease of the reader.

Lemma A.1
.

The set
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm temp}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathcal{R}\right)
is dense in
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{R})
. That is, for every element
\theta
in
\mathcal{R}
, there exists a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
in
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm temp}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathcal{R}\right)
such that
\theta
does not belong to
\mathfrak{p}
. As a result,
we have

(191)

\displaystyle\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm temp}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathcal{R}\right)}\mathfrak{p}=\{0\}.

Proof.

Let
\theta
be an element of
\mathcal{R}
. Since
\mathcal{R}
is a reduced ring, there exists a minimal prime
\eta
such that
\theta\notin\eta
. As a result, the image of
\theta
in the integral domain
\mathcal{R}/\eta
is non-zero. By considering norms over the finite integral extension
\Lambda\hookrightarrow\mathcal{R}/\eta
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{R}
equals
\Lambda
. We will identify
\Lambda
with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket{X_{1},X_{2}}\rrbracket
. Furthermore, we will also work with the natural identification of the power series ring
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket{X_{1},X_{2}}\rrbracket
with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{2}]\!][\![X_{1}]\!]
. Let
\theta
be a non-zero element in
\Lambda
. Let us write

\theta=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}(X_{2})X_{1}^{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{2}]\!][\![X_{1}]\!].

Here,
a_{n}(X_{2})
belongs to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{2}]\!]
. One of the coefficients
a_{n}(X_{2})
must then be non-zero. Without loss of generality, assume that
a_{0}(X_{2})\neq 0
. For each positive integer
k_{2}>3
, the assignment
X_{2}\mapsto(1+p)^{k_{2}}-1
determines a height one prime ideal in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{2}]\!]
. Since
a_{0}(X_{2})
is non-zero, for all but finitely many such assignments with the weight
k_{2}>3
, the element
a_{0}(X_{2})
must map to a non-zero element in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Choose one such ring homomorphism
\alpha_{j_{0}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{2}]\!]\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, with weight
j_{0}\geq M
and
j_{0}\equiv b\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p-1)
along with
\alpha_{j_{0}}(a_{n})\neq 0
, determined by this assignment. Applying
\alpha_{j_{0}}
to each coefficient of
\theta
gives us a non-zero element
\alpha_{j_{0}}(\theta)
in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{1}]\!]
. Once again, we are able to choose a ring homomorphism with
\beta_{k_{0}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{1}]\!]\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, with weight
k_{0}>j_{0}
determined by the assignment
X_{1}\mapsto(1+p)^{k_{0}-1}
, such that
k_{0}\equiv a\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p-1)
and
\beta_{k_{0}}\left(\alpha_{j_{0}}\left(\theta\right)\right)\neq 0
. The kernel of the composite map given below must be
P_{(k_{0},j_{0})}
.

\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{2}]\!][\![X_{1}]\!]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{l_{0}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\![X_{1}]\!]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{k_{0}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p},}
\textstyle{\theta\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\beta_{k_{0}}\left(\alpha_{l_{0}}\left(\theta\right)\right).}

This shows that
\theta
does not belong to
P_{(k_{0},l_{0})}
. The lemma follows.
∎

Theorem A.2
.

There exists a unique continuous pseudocharacter
\mathrm{T}:G_{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}
such that for all primes
\ell
not in
S
, we have
\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell})=T_{\ell}
.

Proof.

The idea of the proof follows the use of pseudocharacters by Wiles
[
89
]
. For each prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
in
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)
, the quotient ring
\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}
is a finitely generated module over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. The quotient topology on
\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}
coincides with the
p
-adic topology. We may thus identify the fraction field of
\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}
with a finite extension
E_{\mathfrak{p}}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Observe that the quotient
\Lambda/(\mathfrak{p}\cap\Lambda)
is canonically identified with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. We endow the infinite direct product

\prod\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)}\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}

with the direct product topology. Using Corollary
4.1
, one can conclude that the intersection
\bigcap\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)}\mathfrak{p}
must equal
\{0\}
. As a result, the diagonal embedding

\displaystyle i:\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\prod\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)}\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}

must be a continuous injective map. Furthermore, since
\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}
is profinite (and hence compact), the image of the diagonal embedding must be closed in
\prod\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)}\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}
. Under the map
i
, the image of
T_{l}
corresponds to

\left(\varphi_{\mathfrak{p}}(T_{l})\right)_{\mathfrak{p}}.

As indicated in Corollary
2.4
, the map
\varphi_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}\hookrightarrow E_{\mathfrak{p}}
corresponds to a classical specialization determined (up to Galois conjugacy) by
\mathfrak{p}
. For each prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
in
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)
, by Theorem
3.1
, we have an associated continuous semi-simple Galois representation
\rho_{\mathfrak{p}}:G_{S}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{4}(E_{\mathfrak{p}})
. This Galois representation is unramified at all primes
\ell
not belonging to
S
. Furthermore,
\mathrm{Trace}(\rho_{\mathfrak{p}})(\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell})
equals
\varphi_{\mathfrak{p}}(T_{l})
. Since the
\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}
’s generate a dense subset of
G_{S}
, the image of the associated trace function must lie in
\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}
, since
\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}
is closed in
E_{\mathfrak{p}}
. The associated trace function
\mathrm{Trace}(\rho_{\mathfrak{p}}):G_{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}
is a continuous pseudocharacter of dimension
4
. Since the dimension of the pseudocharacter remains constant as we vary over the classical primes
\mathfrak{p}
’s, the following function must be a continuous pseudocharacter:

\textstyle{G_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\prod\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)}\mathrm{Trace}(\rho_{\mathfrak{p}})\hskip 8.19447pt}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)}\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p},}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\varphi_{\mathfrak{p}}(T_{l})\right)_{\mathfrak{p}}.}

The image of the
\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}
’s land inside the closed subset
\mathbb{H}
of
\prod\limits_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}^{\rm cls}_{\vartheta}\left(\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)}\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{p}
. Since the
\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}
’s generate a dense subset of
G_{S}
, the image of the entire continuous pseudocharacter lands in
\mathbb{H}
. We get an induced continuous pseudocharacter
\mathrm{T}:G_{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(N,\vartheta)_{\mathfrak{m}}
, such that
\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell})
equals
T_{l}
. The uniqueness of the pseudocharacter follows since the
\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}
’s generate a dense subset of
G_{S}
.
∎
